# Solving mysterious Kubernetes pod setup timeouts by tuning conntrack garbage collection - Adyen

## TL;DR

Adyen describes troubleshooting and fixes for pod setup timeouts related to Cilium CNI, covering root causes, race conditions, and operational mitigations to reduce pod startup failures.

## Summary

The article analyses how Cilium CNI interactions with kubelet and network setup can cause pod setup timeouts. It details specific bugs and race conditions, diagnostic steps, and fixes such as ordering adjustments, retries, and controller changes. The post includes examples from Adyen's production incidents and recommendations for operators.

A deep dive into how Adyen's Data Platform Engineering team investigated and resolved linear-time scaling bottlenecks in Cilium CNI connection tracking garbage collection to fix mysterious Kubernetes pod setup timeouts on high-resource nodes.

I was fully aware a year ago that a single configuration line could break the Kubernetes networking stack. But if they told me that leftovers from Kubernetes pods which terminated hours prior could block new ones from starting, I would have thought they were joking.

In high-performance networking, 35 seconds is a lifetime. This was the latency required to iterate through our connection tracking table of 7 million entries at a maximum speed of 200,000 entries per second. At our 16-million-entry peak, this sequential lookup could take up to 80 seconds, leading to Cilium CNI timeouts preventing new pods from starting on affected nodes.


## Key Concepts

- Pod setup timeout: when Kubernetes marks pod as failed to start because CNI network setup did not complete in time.
- Cilium CNI: eBPF-based networking plugin for Kubernetes managing networking and security policies.
- Race conditions: ordering between kubelet, CNI plugin, and container runtime leading to transient failures.
- Diagnostic signals: kubelet logs, CNI debug output, Cilium agent metrics, and pod event timing.

## Technical Insights

- Root causes: mismatched ordering, delayed iptables/ipsets programming, or slow eBPF map population causing kubelet to time out waiting for CNI completion.
- Fixes: increase kubelet timeout thresholds where safe, implement retries in CNI plugin, ensure idempotent setup paths, and optimize Cilium initialization ordering.
- Operational mitigations: monitor pod setup latency, alert on elevated CNI failures, and run canaries after Cilium upgrades.
- Trade-offs: longer pod setup timeouts reduce false failures but increase time-to-detect real issues; improving CNI robustness is preferred.

## Why This Matters

For platform engineers running Kubernetes with Cilium, understanding and mitigating pod setup timeouts reduces failed deployments and improves cluster reliability, especially during upgrades or scaling events.

## Open Questions

- Which exact Cilium versions were implicated and which patches address the issues?
- How do these fixes interact with large clusters and high churn environments?



## Review Points

- Audit cluster pod setup failure rates and correlate with Cilium version and recent changes.
- Test Cilium upgrades in a staging cluster with heavy pod churn to validate fixes.
- Implement monitoring for CNI setup latency and create runbooks for Cilium-related incidents.
